Then, the differentially encoded bits  are modulated by BPSK.<br>- MSK: The input bit “1” is mapped to frequency  and input bit “0” is mapped to frequency , with , where  is a symbol duration. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509467](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509467.zip) |

|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Initial frequency acquisition procedure is necessary for Device 2b and for Device C, at least for flexible deployment of A-IoT frequency location(s) for outdoor scenario. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508903](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508903.zip) |
| Agreement | Reader identification/differentiation is necessary for Device 2b and for Device C, at least in outdoor scenario to differentiate R2D among different readers. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508903](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508903.zip) |
| Agreement | For initial frequency acquisition for Device 2b and for Device C, a device is expected to determine a frequency point from predefined sync raster points for reception of the periodic synchronization signal.<br>- Details on the sync raster design is handled by RAN4. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508905](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508905.zip) |
| Agreement | - The functionality of broadcast information is to provide the device with at least necessary information for subsequent transmission and reception<br>- On how A-IoT devices acquire the broadcast information, study the following two cases:<br>  - Case B1: Single-step acquisition of the broadcast information<br>  - Case B2: Two-step acquisition of the broadcast information (e.g., MIB-like and then SIB1-like broadcast information)<br>- On whether to use PRDCH to carry the broadcast information,<br>  - For Case B1, PRDCH is used to carry the broadcast information.<br>  - For Case B2<br>    - PRDCH is used to carry the SIB1-like broadcast information.<br>    - A separate PRDCH or a new physical channel dedicated to MIB-like is used to carry the MIB-like broadcast information | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508907](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508907.zip) |
| Agreement | Periodic transmission of broadcast information to provide at least necessary information for subsequent transmission and reception is necessary for Device 2b and for Device C.<br>- FFS (in SI or WI): whether the transmission of broadcast information is strictly periodic at reader<br>- FFS (in SI or WI): periodicity of the transmission of broadcast information | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508907](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508907.zip) |
| Agreement | For the 3 options for the content of the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, study the following aspects:<br>- Recommended scenarios (event-triggered or periodic)<br>- Subsequent transmission(s)/reception(s)/procedure(s) if any after the first D2R transmission for DO-A<br>- Other aspects | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508907](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508907.zip) |
| Agreement | On D2R Tx power control for Device 2b and for Device C, capture the following in TR 38.769.<br>The necessity of D2R Tx power control for Device 2b and Device C for outdoor scenarios were studied and the following observations were provided:<br>- 22 sources (Futurewei, LGE, Spreadtrum, vivo, Nokia, Semtech, CMCC, CEWiT, BOSCH, NEC, InterDigital, TCL, Apple, China Telecom, ZTE, OPPO, Huawei, Docomo, Ericsson, Xiaomi, Samsung, Qualcomm) reported that the D2R Tx power control for Device 2b and Device C is necessary for outdoor scenarios.<br>  - 20 sources (Futurewei, LGE, Spreadtrum, vivo, Nokia, Semtech, CMCC, CEWiT, BOSCH, Quectel, InterDigital, TCL, Apple, OPPO, Huawei, Docomo, Ericsson, Xiaomi, Samsung, ZTE) state that it can reduce the interference between different devices (i.e., the Near-Far problem).<br>  - 14 sources (Futurewei, LGE, Spreadtrum, vivo, Semtech, CMCC, NEC, InterDigital, TCL, Apple, OPPO, Xiaomi, Samsung, ZTE) reported that it helps devices remain available longer.<br>  - 4 sources (Futurewei, Semtech, Apple, Docomo) reported that the interference due to a transmission from a device to a reader can be reduced at an unintended reader<br>  - 1 source (Qualcomm) added that depending on device’s peak power consumption and PA support, the D2R Tx power control may not be feasible.<br>- 1 source (CATT) reported that the D2R Tx power control for Device 2b and Device C is not necessary mentioning in its contribution that the inter-channel interference from different devices is negligible, D2R coverage is limited, and the complexity and the power consumption required at the device side is high. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509587](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509587.zip) |
| Agreement | For reader identification/differentiation at least in outdoor scenario to differentiate R2D among different readers, a device acquires the reader ID-related information by at least one of the following options:<br>- Option 1: Reader ID-related information is contained in the broadcast information.<br>- Option 2: Reader ID-related information is derived from the periodic sync signal sequence(s).<br>- Option 3: Reader ID-related information is carried by L1 R2D control information.<br>- Option 4: Reader ID-related information is contained in the higher-layer information in the paging-like message. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509587](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509587.zip) |
| Agreement | For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, following options are considered feasible.<br>- Option 1: necessary information provided by broadcast information<br>- Option 2: necessary information provided by a paging-like R2D message<br>- Option 3: necessary information derived from a synchronization signal and provided by broadcast information<br>For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, study following option considering feasibility and applicability<br>- Option 4: necessary information provided by device-specific R2D transmission | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509587](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509587.zip) |

| Agreement | For PUSCH repetition scheduled by DCI 0_0 with C-RNTI, inter-slot frequency hopping is supported<br>- Note: intra-slot frequency hopping is not supported unless the repetition time is 1 | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508611.zip) |

| Agreement | For extension of pi/2-BPSK to more MCS entries, down-select one of the following Options:<br>- Option 1：Pi/2-BPSK is extended to all MCS entries in MCS table(s) with spectrum efficiency equals to or smaller than N = 0.8770.<br>  - gNB can configure the maximum MCS up to which pi/2 BPSK is applicable.<br>    - Details on configuration to be discussed further<br>  - FFS: UE signaling to the gNB for this configuration<br>    - FFS: whether/how to provide assistance information and what is the assistance information<br>- Option 2：Support extending Pi/2-BPSK to all MCS entries in MCS table(s) with spectrum efficiency equals to or smaller than X.<br>  - Where X the fixed value select from:<br>    - a) X = 0.8770<br>    - b) X = 0.6016<br>  - Extension is captured in the modified MCS table.<br>  - gNB enables/disables this feature via RRC signalling. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508611.zip) |

## 10.5.1 Evaluation assumptions and performance evaluation

10 Release 20 NR › 10.5 Study on Integrated Sensing And Communication (ISAC) for NR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - In section 6.2 Performance evaluation results in TR 38.765,<br>- Multiple configurations of the following parameters are defined to respectively analyze the evaluation results<br>  - At least Two baseline configurations 1 & 2 are defined for evaluation purpose<br>\|  \| Parameters \| Configuration 1 Baseline \| Configuration 2 Baseline \|<br>\| 1 \| Scenario \| (UMa-AV, 500m) \| (UMa-AV, 500m) \|<br>\| 2 \| Sensing Tx/Rx operating simultaneously or not \| Sensing Tx/Rx operating simultaneously \| Sensing Tx/Rx operating simultaneously \|<br>\| 3 \| Carrier frequency \| 4 or 4.9GHz \| 4 or 4.9GHz \|<br>\| 4 \| Max BS Tx power \| 52 dBm \| 37 dBm \|<br>\| 5 \| BS antenna configuration \| (M, N, P, Mg, Ng, Mp, Np) Tx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) Rx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) = (0.5, 0.8)λ, +45°/-45° polarization \| (M, N, P, Mg, Ng, Mp, Np) Tx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) Rx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) = (0.5, 0.8)λ, +45°/-45° polarization \|<br>\| 6 \| Number of targets per sector in center site \| N=5 \| N=5 \|<br>\| 7 \| Target vertical distribution \| 25-300m \| 25-300m \|<br>  - Additional configurations can be defined for other assumptions of the parameters, based on reported evaluation results in RAN1 #124. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509238](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509238.zip) |
| Agreement | - Companies should report whether a same target is modeled in the ISAC channel of single, multiple or all STXs/SRXs<br>  - Company should report how to determine the single or multiple STXs/SRXs for a target<br>  - The details of STX/SRX determination will be captured along with the corresponding evaluation results in the TR for NR ISAC<br>  - If the evaluation results are derived by measurement reports from multiple/all STXs/SRXs, companies should report how measurement reports from multiple/all STXs/SRXs are used | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509238](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509238.zip) |
| Agreement | From RAN1 perspective, the following measurements that may be reported from RAN are identified in the study of NR ISAC. Note: Down selection of Level(s)/Option(s) for recommendation can be discussed separately.<br>- Level A: Raw data per Tx antenna port per OFDM symbol per RX antenna port per TRP for a given time stamp<br>  - Option A1: Amplitude and phase samples in time/delay domain of the estimated channel, i.e., Amplitude and phase values of channel impulse response<br>  - Option A2: Amplitude and phase per subcarrier in frequency domain of the estimated channel<br>- Level B: Amplitude and phase profile of delay, and/or Doppler, and/or angle per TRP for a given time stamp by using window(s) of the [consecutive] samples in delay, Doppler and/or angle domain<br>  - Option B1: Delay-Doppler profile per Tx antenna port per Rx antenna port, which includes the amplitude and phase samples distributed across different delays and Doppler shifts.<br>  - Option B2: Delay-Angle profile per Tx antenna port per OFDM symbol, which includes the amplitude and phase samples distributed across different delays and spatial angles (e.g., Angle of Arrival).<br>  - Option B3: Delay-Doppler-Angle profile per Tx antenna port, which includes the amplitude and phase samples distributed across delay, Doppler, and angle domains.<br>  - Option B4: Delay profile per Tx antenna port per OFDM symbol per Rx antenna port, which includes the amplitude and phase samples distributed across different delays.<br>  - Note: Level B is applicable for either LCS or GCS<br>- Level C: per detected path/point measurements per Tx antenna port per TRP which may be reflected/scattered from scattering point(s).<br>  - Option C1: Delay/range, Doppler/velocity, one or multiple 3D angles, and [power/confidence metric] per detected path for a given time stamp.<br>    - A path is associated with one couple {delay/range, Doppler/velocity, [power/confidence metric]}, and one or multiple 3D angles.<br>  - Option C2: Doppler/velocity, position, and [power/confidence metric] per detected path for a given time stamp.<br>    - A path is associated with one doppler/velocity, [power/confidence metric], one or multiple positions<br>  - Option C3: Delay/range, Doppler/velocity, 3D angle, and [power/confidence metric] per detected path for a given time stamp.<br>    - A path is associated with one delay, and one or multiple triple {Doppler/velocity, 3D angle, [power/confidence metric]}<br>  - Option C4: Delay/range, Doppler/velocity, 3D angle, and [power/confidence metric] per detected point for a given time stamp<br>    - A point is associated with one range/delay, one Doppler/velocity, one 3D angle<br>  - Option C5: Position, velocity, and [power/confidence metric] per detected point for a given time stamp<br>    - A point is associated with one position, one velocity<br>  - FFS: Velocity can be<br>    - Opt 1: radial velocity<br>    - Opt 2: 3D velocity<br>  - Note:<br>    - Position can be defined in either LCS or GCS<br>    - Angle can be defined in either LCS or GCS<br>    - Ambiguity issue will be further discussed<br>    - 3D angle refers to a pair of horizontal and vertical angles<br>- Level D: Object/target level measurement [per TRP or per gNB]<br>  - One or more value pair(s) {position, velocity} in GCS for a given time stamp is reported for a detected object/target,<br>    - Option D1: Only one value pair {position, velocity} in GCS for a given time stamp is reported for a detected object/target. The association of multiple measurements across different time stamps for the same detected object/target is not reported.<br>    - Option D2: Only one value pair {position, velocity} in GCS for a given time stamp is reported for a detected object/target. The association of multiple measurements across different time stamps for the same detected object/target is reported.<br>    - Option D3: One or more value pairs {position, velocity} in GCS for a given time stamp are reported for a detected object/target. The association of multiple measurements across different time stamps for the same detected object/target is reported.<br>    - Option D4: One or more value pairs {position, velocity} in GCS for a given time stamp are reported for a detected object/target. The association of multiple measurements across different time stamps for the same detected object/target is not reported.<br>  - Note: Ambiguity issue can be further discussed if any<br>  - Note: velocity is 3D velocity for Option D2/D3<br>  - FFS: velocity can be radial velocity or 3D velocity for Option D1/D4<br>- Note: the possible information from RAN to SF for further processing can be further discussed for all levels<br>- Note: the possible information from SF to RAN can be further discussed for all levels<br>- Note: confidence metric can be further discussed for options of Level C/D<br>- Note: definition of time stamp for each of all options of all Levels can be further discussed<br>- For Level A/B/C, Tx antenna port means reference signal antenna port for sensing purpose | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509241](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509241.zip) |
| Agreement | RAN1 confirms the performance objectives in the early agreement from RAN1 #122bis by removing the brackets<br>\| Agreement The following performance objectives are adopted for evaluation purpose of NR ISAC. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509242](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509242.zip) |
| Agreement | Confidence level of the X% represents X percentile point of the cumulative distribution function (CDF) of the estimation errors | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509242](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509242.zip) |
| Conclusion | Sensing service latency and refreshing rate are not considered as performance metrics for evaluation of NR ISAC. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509242](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509242.zip) |

| Agreement | - The following general procedure for performance evaluation of NR ISAC is to be captured in TR 38.765.<br>- Simulation parameter configuration<br>- Sensing scenario generation, including the deployment of sensing Tx/Rx (STXs/SRXs)<br>- Dropping N target(s), where N is equal to 0 or larger than 0<br>- Channel generation and STX/SRX determination for the targets<br>- Sensing signal generation and passing the sensing signal to the generated channel<br>- Sensing signal processing at each SRX, optionally, sensing signal processing based on fusion from multiple STXs/SRXs<br>  - E.g., optionally, sensing signal processing based on tracking<br>- Sensing performance metric calculation.<br>Note: company should report the details on sensing signal processing and ISAC channel generation | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509242](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509242.zip) |
| Agreement | - The following evaluation assumptions for optional FR2-1 carrier frequency are considered for evaluation of NR ISAC<br>\| Parameters \| Values \|<br>\| Scenario \| UMi-AV \|<br>\| Carrier Frequency \| 30 GHz \|<br>\| System bandwidth \| 400 MHz \|<br>\| SCS \| 120 kHz \|<br>\| Inter-BS (2D) distance \| 200 m \|<br>\| BS antenna configurations \| (M,N,P,Mg,Ng;Mp,Np) = (16,16,2,2,1;1,1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5 or 0.8)λ, +45°/-45° polarization for whole panel; (16,16,2,1,1;1,1) for Tx and (16,16,2,1,1;1,1) for Rx \|<br>\| Beam set at TRxP \| Up to company report \|<br>\| Antenna isolation \| 80 ~ 100 dB \|<br>\| Max BS Tx power \| 30 dBm \|<br>\| BS noise figure \| 7 dB \|<br>- Up to company to report evaluation results and additional configuration information for FR2-1. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509242](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509242.zip) |

eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508467](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508467.zip) |
| Agreement | For the evaluation of GNSS resilient NR-NTN operation, consider the following solutions at least for initial access procedure at least to increase PRACH tolerance and/or reducing time/frequency uncertainty:<br>- Solution 1A: Multiple PRACH transmissions (e.g. with different roots or different formats or with different time/frequency pre-compensation using multiple reference locations within the uncertainty area) using existing PRACH formats<br>- Solution 1B: New PRACH restricted sets.<br>- Solution 1C: Single PRACH transmission with multiple TAC/FAC commands in single/multiple RAR MAC PDU scheduling multiple msg3 with different TA/FA.<br>- Solution 1D: Signalling enhancements for Msg2/Msg4 (e.g. enhanced TA command, frequency adjustment command, reference point adjustment command).<br>- Solution 1E: configuration/selection of a set of existing PRACH root for a Single PRACH transmission.<br>- Solution 1F: PRACH transmission using TA margin indicated by Network and RO masking.<br>- Solution 1G: Adaptation of PRACH configuration.<br>- Solution 2A: Single/multi-satellite DL-TDOA based on current specifications.<br>- Solution 2B: Multiple random access attempts based on different time/frequency pre-compensation hypotheses (e.g. based on multiple reference points within the uncertainty area)<br>- Solution 2C: Solutions based on broadcasting DL timestamp(s).<br>- Solution 2D: UE side time/frequency pre-compensation based on reference location or TA/Doppler compensation information provided by gNB.<br>- Solution 2E: service link time/frequency pre-compensation based on last acquired GNSS position<br>- Solution 2F: UE side time/frequency pre-compensation based on reference signal.<br>- Solution 3: Implementation-based techniques e.g. using a long enough PRACH processing window and multiple timing hypotheses for PRACH preamble reception with large max differential delay.<br>Other Solutions are not precluded.<br>As part of the evaluation of the solutions above, at least the following aspects should be assessed:<br>- specification impact when relevant (including whether the solutions remain within the scope of the SID)<br>- performance,<br>- applicability to different scenarios,<br>- complexity,<br>- Coexistence with legacy UEs<br>- the signalling overhead. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508469.zip) |
| Agreement | With respect solution 2A, for the evaluation of DL-only UE-based positioning method, using DL-TDOA, consider the following evaluation assumptions and parameters:<br>\| Parameter \| Description/Value \|<br>\| Satellite Orbit \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| Satellite parameters \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| Channel model/ Delay spread \| Based on section 6.7.2 of TR 38.811 \|<br>\| FR/Carrier frequency \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| BW \| To be reported by companies. Baseline: 10 MHz for FR1 and 50 MHz for FR2 \|<br>\| Subcarrier spacing \| 15 kHz for FR1-NTN, 120 kHz for FR2-NTN \|<br>\| Number of satellites in view \| Case 1: 1 Satellite Case 2: At least 2 Satellites \|<br>\| UE type \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| Positioning signals \| DL-PRS \|<br>\| Reference Signal Physical Structure and Resource Allocation (RE pattern) \| DL: Comb-2 \|<br>\| Number of symbols used per occasion \| 6 \|<br>\| Number of DL-PRS occasions \| To be reported. \|<br>\| Time window for measurement collection \| To be reported. \|<br>\| UE speed \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| UE minimum elevation angle \| For Case 1, as per RAN1#122 agreement. For Case 2, to be reported. \|<br>\| Ue clock drift/error \| To be reported \|<br>\| Performance metrics \| UE 2D as baseline. 3D can be reported by companies. Residual UL timing and frequency error after applying pre-compensation based on positioning \|<br>\| UE position estimation algorithme \| To be reported. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508469.zip) |
| Agreement | The skeleton for TR 38.742 proposed in R1-2508479 is endorsed in R1-2509611 with the following updates in red:<br>\| 4 Background and motivation 4.1 Review of previous NR-NTN releases 4.2 Needs for GNSS resilience NR NTN operation 4.3 Use cases and deployment scenarios 5 Assumptions and study parameters 5.1 GNSS availability scenarios 5.2 System assumptions 5.3 Evaluation scenarios and parameters 6 Evaluation of impacts of GNSS temporary unavailability 6.1 Impact on synchronization in initial access 6.2 Impact on synchronization in connected mode 7 Evaluation of solutions for GNSS resilient NR-NTN 7.1 Potential solutions for initial access 7.1.1 Evaluation of solutions 7.2 Potential solutions for RRC connected mode 7.2.1 Evaluation of solutions \|<br>\| 8 Conclusions and recommendations 8.1 Summary of findings 8.2 Open issues and future work 8.3 Recommendations for normative work \| | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2508469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2508469.zip) |
| Agreement | To assess the impacts on connected mode during NR-NTN GNSS-resilient operation, evaluate the signalling overhead for a UE in RRC Connected mode under the following assumptions:<br>\| Parameter \| Description/Value \|<br>\| Satellite Orbit \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| Satellite parameters \| As per RAN1#122 agreement \|<br>\| Cell type \| Quasi-Earth Fixed cell Moving cell may be considered and reported. \|<br>\| Range of elevation angle \| Baseline: From 90 degree to minimum elevation angle. Other values outside orbital plane to be reported. In this case, starting point is the highest elevation angle, and ending at the minimum elevation angle. \|<br>\| UE pre-compensation of RTT/Doppler on the service link \| Case 1: At least for Scenario 1, based on assistance information provided by the Network Case 2: For Scenario 2, based on at least the last known UE location Details to be reported. Other pre-compensation methods can be reported by companies. \|<br>\| Duty cycle \| Baseline: Continuous UL transmission every slot Other approaches if used, to be reported. \|<br>\| UE speed \| Baseline: 3 km/h To be reported \|<br>\| Timing tolerance when connected (Note 1) \| To be reported \|<br>\| Frequency tolerance when connected (Note 1) \| To be reported \|<br>\| Initial timing error \| Baseline: 0 To be reported \|<br>\| Initial frequency error \| Baseline: 0 To be reported \|<br>\| Other \| To be reported Note: Details of deriving UL timing and frequency error at gNB to be reported. \|<br>\| Performance metrics \| Minimum period between two consecutive adjustment commands (in seconds) Maximum period between two consecutive adjustment commands (in seconds) Average number of adjustment commands per second Average number of times frequency error exceeds tolerance limit per second Average number of times timing error exceeds tolerance limit per second Other metrics may be considered and reported. \|<br>\| Note 1: A timing and/or frequency correction is issued if the timing and frequency error exceeds tolerance limits and , respectively. \| Note 1: A timing and/or frequency correction is issued if the timing and frequency error exceeds tolerance limits and , respectively. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509609](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509609.zip) |
| Conclusion | A Post-meeting Email discussion for Rel-20 NR-NTN is assigned for the following tasks:<br>- Collection of companies evaluation results for PRACH performance.<br>  - Note: The spreadsheet for PRACH performance evaluation embedded in R1-2509609 is used to collect companies’ results.<br>- Endorsement of a template for collection of results for Connected mode.<br>Duration of Email discussion: 2nd to 4th of February 2026.<br>Moderator: THALES (Mohamed) | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>[R1-2509609](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Docs/R1-2509609.zip) |

## 11.1 Overview of 6GR air interface

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | If the minimum spectrum allocation is 3MHz with 15kHz SCS for 6GR,<br>- Opt1: Design of the common signals/channels (at least for SSB) for initial access by assuming bandwidth larger than 3MHz, which is applicable to any spectrum allocations with adjustment, if applicable<br>- Opt2: A single design of the common signals/channels (at least for SSB) for initial access by assuming minimum spectrum allocation as target bandwidth 3MHz, which is applicable to any spectrum allocations | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | Skeleton for TR 38.760-1 “Study on 6G Radio RAN1 aspects” v0.0.3 in R1-2509569 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - For scalable 6GR design for diverse device types, RAN1 can at least consider the following, targeting applicable to all 6G device types,<br>  - Basic initial access procedures from RAN1 perspective<br>  - Other PHY features after initial access procedure, e.g., Other DL/UL control, scheduling/HARQ<br>  - Coverage features to meet the identified coverage target<br>  - Energy saving both at BS and UE sides<br>  - MRSS<br>  - Note: whether these features are supported, mandatory or optional is separate discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”,<br>  - The link budget template candidates 1 and 2 are used to calculate the metric(s) as starting point to compare existing 5G mid-band and 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz, with potential future update.<br>  - During initial access/random access<br>    - Coverage target is referring the bottleneck channel (i.e. Rel-15 NR Msg3) during initial access/random access for existing 5G mid-band<br>  - FFS target value(s) of data rate for data channels relative to 5G mid-band<br>  - Following deployment scenarios are considered<br>    - Urban macro (both O2I and outdoor)<br>    - Sub-urban macro (both O2I and outdoor)<br>  - Following carrier frequencies are considered to calculate the metric(s)<br>    - [4 GHz] as the existing 5G mid-band<br>    - 7 GHz as 6G deployment<br>  - Template in R1-2509615 is to be used for collecting inputs on the values from companies.<br>[Post-123-R20-6GR-Overall] – Shinya (DCM)<br>Email discussion for collecting inputs on the values from companies for link budget template, Jan 26th until 30th | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - For the smallest maximum supported RF and BB UE BW without spectrum aggregation for at least one low-tier device type supported by 6GR framework, from physical layer perspective, RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Overall device complexity<br>  - Overall system performance impact<br>  - Energy efficiency for both BS and UE<br>  - Aim at a single common signals/channels design in idle mode and initial access for diverse device types, as well as meeting mobile broadband service requirements as high priority | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |

| Agreement | For FTP Model 3, the packet delay budget (PDB) can be additionally considered,<br>- The latency characteristic of the traffic in RAN side (i.e., air interface) is modelled as packet delay budget (PDB). The PDB is a limited time budget for a packet to be transmitted over the air from a BS to a UE for DL, or from a UE to a BS for UL.<br>- For a given packet, the delay of the packet incurred in air interface is measured from the time that the packet arrives at the BS to the time that it is successfully transferred to the UE for DL, or from a UE to a BS for UL. If the delay is larger than a given PDB for the packet, the packet is said to violate PDB, otherwise the packet is said to be successfully delivered.<br>- Values for PDB, e.g., {10ms, 20ms, 30ms, 50ms, 100ms, 200ms, 300ms, 1000ms, 2000ms} can be considered.<br>- Which values will be used will consider the use case for the evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| Agreement | For traffic model(s) for AI/ML services, the following can be considered:<br>- Packet size:<br>  - How to model the packet size, a fixed one or multiple values, or modelled as a random variable.<br>- Packet arrival:<br>  - FFS the details to determine the packet arrival rate, e.g.,<br>    - N multiple packets arrive together as a burst. The burst interval time is modelled as a random variable.<br>      - Within the burst, the N packets arrive according to a statistical distribution.<br>    - Packets arrive separately.<br>  - FFS whether/how to model the Jitter and the relation with the packet arrival.<br>- FFS: Whether the packet importance is known. The PDB is a limited time budget for a packet to be transmitted over the air from a base station to a UE, or from a UE to a base station<br>- FFS Whether/how to consider the Packet success rate requirement: [xx%] and the relation with the PDB.<br>- FFS how to model different cases, e.g., image-based GenAI, video-based GenAI, and chatbot, etc.<br>- FFS: Whether/how other traffic models (e.g., XR, FTP1/3) can be used to reflect above characteristics.<br>Note: input from SA4 if any will be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

## 11.4.2 Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.4 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For PS/GS fixed MCS performance reporting for 10% BLER (other target x% BLER can also be reported), adopt the following format for simulation as a starting point for result reporting.<br>\| NR reference \| NR reference \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \|<br>\| SE \| (Mod order, coding rate) \| SE point specific parameters \| Baseline (uniform QAM) SNR to reach target BLER \| Gain/loss in dB wrt NR baseline at target BLER x% \|<br>\| SE x \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE y \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE z \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE point independent assumptions \| SE point independent assumptions \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \|<br>Note: For NR MCS reference, since NR has multiple MCS tables, it is not enough to provide the MCS index. Instead, need to provide the (modulation order, coding rate) pair for the simulated SE<br>Note: For SE point specific parameters:<br>- For GS, this can be a pointer to the constellation used for this SE point<br>- For PS, this can be a constellation size, coding rate and shaping parameter used for this SE point<br>- Note: Other metrics (at least complexity) will be merged into the same table with other columns, if details of the metrics are agreeable.<br>- Note: For AMC study, if possible, we can use the same table format | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | On how to evaluate complexity, storage requirement, delay and parallelism/serialism for PS and GS compared with uniform QAM.<br>- For PS<br>  - The demapper complexity is compared with uniform QAM demapper complexity<br>    - Can report the demapper complexity with PS and the demapper complexity of NR MCS with the same spectrum efficiency, and the ratio of the complexities<br>      - Also report the number of spatial layers, dm-algorithm used and the receiver type (e.g., LMMSE or rML), and fixed point assumed or floating point assumed.<br>  - The Distribution Matcher (DM)/Distribution De-Matcher (DDM) complexity and/or storage requirement as a function of the DM algorithm used (ESS, CCDM, etc), precision of fixed point implementation, block length, and the number of bit levels shaped per symbol<br>    - For complexity, can report the complexity normalized by the number of information bits<br>      - As a reference, can also report the computation complexity of LDPC decoding with 10 iterations.<br>    - For storage requirement, can report the overall storage needed for DM/DDM for supporting all MCS in the MCS table and all shaping related parameters<br>    - DM and DDM complexity and storage will be counted separately<br>  - The DM/DDM processing delay, parallelism/serialism, as a function of DM design and block length, and their impact to throughput<br>- For GS,<br>  - The demapper complexity is compared with uniform QAM demapper complexity<br>    - Can report the ratio of GS demapper complexity over the uniform QAM demapper complexity<br>      - Also report if 1D-NUC or 2D-NUC is used, # of spatial layers, and the receiver type (e.g., LMMSE or rML)<br>    - Also need to report the assumption on complexity counting, e.g, fixed point assumed or floating point assumed<br>  - The storage requirement for storing all the constellations corresponding to the MCS indices in the MCS table, as a function of precision of constellation point storage<br>  - Processing delay and parallelism/serialism, if applicable, and their impact to throughput<br>Note: the complexity is represented by the numbers of comparison, addition/subtraction, and multiplication/division operations, normalized by the number of information bits.<br>Note: For complexity as a function of SE point, will add a column in the already agreed performance reporting table.<br>Note: For complexity/storage not as a function of SE point, will add a row in the already agreed performance reporting table.<br>Note: Spec impact will be separately evaluated, include BICM, scrambling, etc<br>Note:<br>For 4K uniform QAM DL and 1K uniform QAM UL link level performance study, the following format can be used for performance reporting.<br>\| (modOrder, coding rate*1024, SE) \| Assumed TX/RX EVM \| Channel 1 \| Channel 2 \| Channel 3 \|<br>\| (modOrder, coding rate*1024, SE) \| Assumed TX/RX EVM \| SNR to achieve target BLER \| SNR to achieve target BLER \| SNR to achieve target BLER \|<br>\| (10, 900.5, 8.7939) for DL evaluation (8, 916.5, 7.1602) for UL evaluation \| Legacy EVM for 1K QAM for DL and 256QAM for UL respectively \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| (10, 948, 9.2578) for DL evaluation (8, 948, 7.4063) for UL evaluation \| Legacy EVM for 1K QAM for DL and 256 QAM for UL respectively \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE1 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE2 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE3 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Other parameters \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>- For assumed TX/RX EVM, before we receive any concrete numbers from RAN4, companies can provide their assumptions. One example can be 6dB tighter than the EVM of 1K QAM for DL and 256QAM for UL.<br>- Other parameters include: Channel estimation assumption (genie or realistic), channel configurations (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO and TX/RX antenna configurations, channel types, number of spatial layers,), assumed residual freq offset range, number of allocated RBs, etc<br>- Two highest MCS points in DL 1K QAM and UL 256QAM in NR added in the table for comparison.<br>- This is preliminary result and not intended for TR<br>Note: For high order uniform QAM for DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM, to provide the UPT with the high order QAM (DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM) over the UPT without the high order QAM under the assumed deployment scenario. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

Instead, need to provide the (modulation order, coding rate) pair for the simulated SE<br>Note: For SE point specific parameters:<br>- For GS, this can be a pointer to the constellation used for this SE point<br>- For PS, this can be a constellation size, coding rate and shaping parameter used for this SE point<br>- Note: Other metrics (at least complexity) will be merged into the same table with other columns, if details of the metrics are agreeable.<br>- Note: For AMC study, if possible, we can use the same table format | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | To evaluate the proposal to allow a single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple MCS entries (with different modulation order and coding rate combinations with uniform QAM or with different shaping parameters, coding rate, and constellation size combinations for PS and different coding rate and constellation combinations for GS).<br>- When providing results, to provide the following information<br>  - Details on the design of MCS table with overlapping MCS entries and expected size of MCS table, including performance comparison of designs with the same expected size of MCS table<br>  - Performance benefit under different channel and rank assumptions<br>    - As baseline, provide performance with legacy MCS table up to 256 QAM<br>      - Can additionally provide performance with legacy MCS table up to 1K QAM<br>    - For PS/GS, provide performance allowing each SE point to be mapped to one or more MCS entries<br>      - For PS/GS, provide performance allowing each SE point to be mapped to only one MCS entry (from the set of one or more MCS entries)<br>    - For uniform QAM, provide performance allowing each SE point to be mapped to one or more MCS entries<br>  - MCS selection mechanism across multiple MCS corresponding to the same spectrum efficiency.<br>    - If UE feedback is needed for gNB to select between multiple MCS entries corresponding to the same SE, provide details on what is to be fed back<br>- FFS: How different MPR for different modulation order is captured in the simulation for uplink<br>- FFS: How different EVM for different modulation order is captured in the simulation<br>- For the purpose of this study, the same set of SE points as in legacy uniform QAM table will be used as starting point.<br>- When reporting performance, also report other assumptions, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc<br>- To propose how to align shaping parameters or how to align coding rate for facilitating comparison. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 -
